God, or Nature, alone remains, while minds and bodies become modes under Thought and Extension. The gain is unity and the dissolution of interaction, since the order of ideas is the order of things. The cost is necessitarianism and the threat of acosmism.

**Leibniz** accepts that extended composites are not ultimate but refuses to sacrifice individuals. Simplicity, perception and appetition define infinitely many monads; the identity of indiscernibles guarantees their uniqueness. The gain is active plurality and internal agency. The cost is that bodies become well-founded phenomena and that correspondence requires pre-established harmony and a divine selector.

| Movement | Problem solved | New problem generated |
|---|---|---|
| Descartes → Spinoza | dependence and interaction between created substances | finite individuality and contingency weaken |
| Spinoza → Leibniz | restores genuine individual centres of activity | harmony and complete concepts strain explanation |

⚠️ The movement is therefore neither linear progress nor arbitrary disagreement. Each successor enforces one rationalist demand more strictly and pays elsewhere, because coherence, individuality and experiential plausibility cannot all be maximised at once.

**Verdict.** Rationalist substance evolves through **internal criticism**, and the lesson is conceptual rather than historical: the definition of the basic unit already contains a theory of causation, of individuality and of freedom, so choosing a criterion of substance is choosing a metaphysics. That is also why the movement terminates only when Locke and Hume attack the innate resources which made the vocabulary of substance available in the first place.

##### Why this earns marks

- Analyses a movement rather than describing three positions.
- Attaches a named gain and a named cost to each stage.
- Uses a compact grid to make the dialectic visible.
- Concludes at the conceptual level, which is what "analyse" demands.

#### Original 6 · 20 marks

**Question:** Compare the roles assigned to God in the systems of Descartes, Spinoza and Leibniz, and assess which system depends on God most heavily. Critically discuss.

##### Independent model answer

The three systems assign God three different jobs, and the depth of dependence is not the same in each.

**Descartes: God guarantees.** The *cogito* yields one certainty. Divine veracity, established by the trademark and conservation proofs of Meditation III and the ontological proof of Meditation V, converts a criterion read off that single case into a general licence, and then underwrites memory, extended reasoning and the inference from involuntary sensory ideas to an external world.

**Spinoza: God is the system.** God, or Nature, is the one absolutely infinite substance; Thought and Extension are attributes expressing its whole essence; minds and bodies are modes. *Ethics* I, P15 makes everything exist in God, and P18 makes God the immanent, not the transitive, cause. Nothing is produced outside God, so nothing needs to be certified from outside.

**Leibniz: God selects and harmonises.** The Principle of Sufficient Reason demands a necessary being outside the contingent series; God surveys infinitely many compossible worlds and actualises the most perfect — the greatest variety under the simplest laws — and arranges all monads at creation so that their internally unfolding states correspond.

⚠️ **The assessment.** Dependence must be measured by what collapses if the divine premise fails. Remove Descartes' God and the system contracts to the momentary *cogito*: mathematics, memory and the world all go. Remove Leibniz's God and there is no reason why *this* compossible set is actual and no source of the harmony, so the correspondence between mind and body becomes a brute coincidence. Remove Spinoza's God and there is no system left to have lost anything — but that is because God is not a premise within the system, it is the system.

The paradox is instructive. Spinoza's God is the most **pervasive** and the least **vulnerable**, because it is not inferred from a concept of perfection; this is why Kant's objection that existence is not a real predicate bears directly on Descartes and Leibniz and leaves Spinoza largely untouched.

**Verdict.** Judged by fragility, **Descartes** depends on God most heavily, since a single contested proof carries his entire epistemology; judged by pervasiveness, Spinoza does, since God is not a support but the substance of everything asserted. Naming which sense of "depends" is in use is what turns this comparison into an argument.

##### Why this earns marks

- Uses one matched axis — the job God performs — across all three systems.
- Applies a genuine test: what collapses if the divine premise is removed.
- Distinguishes two senses of "depends" and rules on both.
- Adds Kant only as a one-line rider, respecting the ownership boundary.

## OPTIONAL ADVANCED DEPTH — NOT REQUIRED FOR A CORE ANSWER

### Prerequisite check before using this block

Nothing below is needed to write a complete answer to any of the fourteen routed questions. Use it only after the twelve Basic sessions, the thirty-two diagnostics and the solved practice are secure, and then only as a single evaluative rider inside a fifteen- or twenty-mark answer. Every item here is a **live scholarly dispute** and is marked ❓ accordingly; presenting any of these readings as settled doctrine would be a mistake in the opposite direction from omitting them.

### A1. The Cartesian-circle literature ❓

- **The issue:** does Descartes need divine veracity *before* he may trust clear and distinct perception at all, or only to secure it across memory and extended reasoning?
- **Competing readings:** the vicious-circle reading in Arnauld's line; the **memory-guarantee** reading, on which God certifies only perceptions no longer attended to; and a stronger anti-circle reading on which the *cogito* and certain simple intuitions are self-certifying while being attended to.
- **The further objection and the counter-reply:** even the proofs of God appear to rely on premises whose reliability is in question. The counter-reply is that present clear intuition compels assent immediately, while God guarantees its lasting trustworthiness once it is no longer before the mind.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** one evaluative line after the standard presentation of method and God. It sharpens the evaluation; it must not replace the rebuilding sequence.
- **Do not over-study:** the full apparatus of the Objections and Replies is not marks-essential here.

### A2. Spinoza's attributes problem ❓

- **The issue:** if substance has infinitely many attributes but human beings know only Thought and Extension, what exactly *is* an attribute, and how is our knowledge of it limited?
- **Competing readings:** the **epistemic-limitation** reading, on which we grasp two of infinitely many genuinely real attributes; the **expressive** reading, on which attributes are distinct ways in which one substance is intelligible; and a stricter conceptual reading that resists treating attributes as quasi-parts of God.
- **The further objection and the counter-reply:** unknown infinite attributes can look ornamental, since the working system runs on two. The counter-reply is that their infinity blocks anthropomorphic reduction and secures the claim that substance is absolutely self-caused and not exhausted by human cognition.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** one line in a Spinoza substance or God answer, to show why Thought and Extension are not Cartesian substances in disguise.

### A3. Leibniz on contingency by infinite analysis ❓

- **The issue:** if the complete individual concept contains all of a subject's predicates, how can contingent truth and freedom survive?
- **Competing readings:** the **compatibilist** reading, on which truths are certain for God yet contingent because their opposite is possible in another world; a **near-necessitarian** reading; and a modal reading that ties contingency to God's free choice of the best world.
- **The technical device:** necessary truths terminate in identities through a **finite** analysis; contingent truths require an **infinite** analysis that only God completes. This lets Leibniz say that a contingent predicate is contained in a complete concept without being logically necessary.
- **The residual difficulty:** contingency can look **merely epistemic** from the creature's standpoint — real for God's infinite analysis, unavailable to the agent.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** an evaluative rider in a freedom or determinism answer, after pre-established harmony has already been explained clearly.

### A4. Pantheism, panentheism and the labelling dispute ❓

- **The issue:** is "pantheist" or "panentheist" the more accurate description of Spinoza's God?
- **The case for pantheism:** nothing exists outside God; all things are modes within one immanent substance; *Deus sive Natura* identifies the two terms.
- **The case for panentheism:** substance possesses infinitely many attributes and is not exhausted by the two humans know, so God exceeds the finite modes even while containing them.
- **The third position:** Hegel's **acosmism** charge, which is not a label for Spinoza's theism at all but an accusation that the finite world disappears.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** in the 2022 answer, declare the qualified-pantheist verdict and note the panentheist alternative in one clause. Do not spend a paragraph on nomenclature.

### A5. The analogical predication of "substance" in Descartes ❓

- **The issue:** is Descartes' use of "substance" for God and for created things an equivocation that undermines the system, or a controlled analogy?
- **The charitable reading:** the *Principles* I.51 distinction is deliberate. "Substance" applies absolutely to God and relatively to created things, in the way scholastic analogical predication permits.
- **The hostile reading:** the definition is then doing no work, since anything that depends on only one thing may be called independent.
- **Why it matters:** the answer determines whether Spinoza is Descartes' **corrector** or his **opponent**, which changes the tone of every 2018-style answer.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** one sentence, and only in a twenty-mark substance question.

### A6. Is pre-established harmony unfalsifiable? ❓

- **The issue:** harmony explains any observed correspondence equally well, so critics argue it is not an explanation at all.
- **Leibniz's defence:** it is the only account that avoids both unintelligible cross-substance causation and the collapse of plurality into one substance, and unlike occasionalism it requires no continuous miracle.
- **The residual:** the defence is comparative, not positive — it shows harmony is preferable to two rivals, not that it is independently confirmable.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** as the residual column of the criticism ledger, never as the main criticism.

### Conceptual links — not current-affairs items

Static early-modern philosophy does not require a manufactured contemporary anchor, and none has been forced here. The genuine conceptual links, usable as a closing sentence when a question invites breadth, are: the substance question running forward into Locke, Hume and Kant; the mind–body problem as the ancestor of contemporary debates about mental causation; and the identity of indiscernibles as a live principle in later logic and metaphysics. Each belongs to a **later owner** and should be named in a clause, not developed.

### Source trail for this block

Descartes, *Meditations* and the Fourth Objections and Replies; *Principles of Philosophy* I.51; the 1643 correspondence with Princess Elisabeth of Bohemia. Spinoza, *Ethics* I–II and V; Letter 50 to Jarig Jelles. Leibniz, *Discourse on Metaphysics*, *Monadology*, *Theodicy* and the Leibniz–Clarke correspondence. The interpretive positions summarised above follow the repository's advanced Western Philosophy dossier, which names Arnauld, Curley, Della Rocca, Adams and Jolley as the standard modern discussants.

## CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

> Compressed revision notes for the complete topic, arranged by examinable structure rather than by a fixed template. Read these last, and read them fast. Parts A to L cover every Basic session; the optional advanced material is deliberately excluded, because none of it is required for a core answer.

### A. Rationalist operating logic — the five tenets

- **Warrant, not hostility to the senses.** Experience may occasion thought; only intellect certifies necessity and universality.
- Five tenets: **innate cognitive resources** (dispositional, not preformed propositions) · **reason grounds necessity** · **intuition and deduction** (Descartes intuition and order; Spinoza geometrical demonstration; Leibniz identities and sufficient reasons) · **the real is the rational** · **substance metaphysics**.
- One inherited definition: substance = **in itself, conceived through itself, needing nothing else**.
- The dial: **loose → 3 · strict → 1 · strict plus simplicity → ∞**.
- Memory chain: **attribute separates → independence unifies → simplicity multiplies.**
- Whole-topic line: *method shared; substance criterion diverges; God, mind–body and freedom follow the substance choice.*

### B. Cartesian demolition and reconstruction spine

```text
four rules -> methodic doubt -> cogito -> clear and distinct rule ->
God -> divine veracity -> mathematics + external world -> dualism
```

- Four rules (*Discourse* II): evidence · analysis · order · complete enumeration. Rule 1 does the damage.
- Doubt in three cumulative stages: **sense-deception → dream argument → evil genius** *(genius malignus)*.
- *Meditations* wording: **"I am, I exist, is necessarily true whenever it is put forward by me or conceived in my mind."** The Latin tag *cogito, ergo sum* is from the *Discourse* and *Principles* I.7.
- Status: **immediate intuition**, performative, present-tense; **not** a syllogism.
- Limit: thinking existence, **not** enduring substance or diachronic identity. Lichtenberg and Hume attack exactly here.
- The **Archimedean point**: one fixed place from which everything else is moved.

### C. Ideas, innateness and the Locke exchange

- Three kinds: **adventitious** (involuntary, seemingly from outside) · **factitious** (voluntarily constructed) · **innate** (native to rational nature).
- Criterion of truth: **clear** = present to an attentive mind; **distinct** = containing nothing unclear. Derived from the *cogito*, not assumed.
- Argument from insufficiency: **infinity · substance · mathematical necessity** cannot be copied from finite sensation. Objective reality of an idea needs a cause with adequate formal reality.
- Innateness is **dispositional** — seeds of knowledge; a constitution, not a content.
- Locke, *Essay* I: (i) no **universal assent**; (ii) nothing unknown can be **in the mind**; (iii) **sensation and reflection** suffice.
- Adjudication: occurrent innatism **falls**; dispositional innatism **stands but owes a criterion** distinguishing it from a bare capacity to learn.

### D. The wax argument and certain knowledge

- Catalogue destroyed by fire: taste · scent · colour · shape · size · hardness · coldness · sound.
- Same wax still judged to remain ⇒ not any sensible item; not **imagination**, since the possible shapes are indefinite — *flexibile et mutabile*.
- ∴ grasped by **mental scrutiny** *(inspectio mentis)* as an **extended, flexible, changeable** thing.
- Paired case: **"hats and coats"** — what passes for seeing is judging.
- Result: bodies known by understanding; **mind is *notior***, in the strict sense of *more indubitable*.
- Presuppositions: bearer metaphysics (P1) · sense exhausted by the catalogue (P2) · imagination image-bound (P3) · perception self-intimating (P4).
- ❌ The wax **does not prove that bodies exist**.

### E. God in Descartes — three proofs, one objection, one world

| Proof | Meditation | Move |
|---|---|---|
| Trademark / causal | III | objective reality of the idea of the infinite needs an adequate formal cause |
| Conservation | III | I cannot sustain myself from moment to moment |
| Ontological | V | existence belongs to the concept of a supremely perfect being |

- Divine veracity ⇒ **general reliability** of clear and distinct perception.
- **Cartesian Circle** (Arnauld, Fourth Objections): criterion proves God; God certifies criterion. **Reply:** attended intuition compels; God secures **memory** and **long chains**. **Residual:** the proofs themselves use remembered premises.
- External world (Meditation VI): involuntary ideas → **passive faculty** → needs an **active cause** → **great natural inclination** to bodies → **no correcting faculty** → a non-deceiver could not permit it → **bodies exist, as extended**.
- Secured: body and extension. **Not** secured: exact colours, sizes and distances.
- Verdict line: *the* Meditations *stand or fall on Meditation III, not Meditation II.*

### F. Substance — one criterion, three enforcements

| | Descartes | Spinoza | Leibniz |
|---|---|---|---|
| Count | 3: God + mind + matter | 1: God, or Nature | ∞ monads |
| Criterion enforced | real distinction by principal attribute | absolute independence | simplicity + internal activity |
| Definition applied | **analogically** (*Principles* I.51) | rigorously | rigorously, plus partlessness |
| The individual | a thinking substance, or a mode of extension | a **mode** of God | a **substance** — each monad unique |
| The physical world | *res extensa*, mechanistic | a mode of the attribute Extension | **well-founded phenomenon** |
| Cost | equivocation; interaction problem | individuality and contingency weaken | correspondence bought from God |

- Best twenty-mark thesis: **the common definition does not force a common classification; the enforcement does.**

### G. Spinoza as one integrated system

- **I, Def. 7** — free = exists from the necessity of its own nature, determined to action by itself alone.
- **I, P15** — whatever is, is in God (ontological "in", not spatial).
- **I, P16** — infinitely many things in infinitely many ways follow from divine necessity.
- **I, P18** — God is the **immanent**, not the transitive, cause.
- **I, P29** — nothing in nature is contingent.
- **I, P33** — things could have been produced in no other way or order.
- **II, P7** — the order and connection of ideas is the same as the order and connection of things.
- **II, P13** — the human mind is the **idea of** the human body.
- **II, P40 Sch. 2** — three kinds of knowledge; **II, P41** — the first kind is the only cause of falsity; **II, P43** — *verum index sui*.
- **III, P6–P7** — striving *(conatus)* is the actual essence of a thing.
- **V, P25** — intuitive knowledge is the mind's highest endeavour; **V, P32 Cor.** — the intellectual love of God.
- Knowledge ladder: **imagination** (*experientia vaga*, *ex signis*) → **reason** (common notions) → **intuitive knowledge**. Example: **merchant · mathematician · seer**; the fourth proportional is **6**.
- Vocabulary: *causa sui* · *Deus sive Natura* (IV, Preface) · *natura naturans* / *natura naturata* · *sub specie aeternitatis* · *amor Dei intellectualis* · *beatitudo*.
- **"Determination is negation"** — **Letter 50 to Jarig Jelles, 2 June 1674**; universalised by **Hegel**. Not a sentence of the *Ethics*.
- Traps: not an atheist · attributes are not substances · God does not choose · pantheism ≠ acosmism.

### H. Leibniz — plurality without interaction

- Argument to simples: extension → divisible → composite → aggregates presuppose **partless units**.
- Monad: simple · unextended · indestructible · **perception** (many represented in one) + **appetition** (internal transition).
- **Minute perceptions** *(petites perceptions)* below awareness — the roar of the sea. **Apperception** only in rational spirits.
- Hierarchy: **bare entelechies** → **animal souls** (memory) → **rational spirits** (apperception, necessary truths, moral community with God). **God** is the unique **uncreated** necessary simple substance, not the top of the created series.
- ***Monadology* §7** — "monads have no windows", as it is usually rendered *(les monades n'ont point de fenêtres)*.
- **Identity of indiscernibles** — duplicates would leave God without a sufficient reason.
- **Dominant monad** + organic aggregate; bodies are **well-founded phenomena** *(phaenomena bene fundata)*.
- **Principle of Sufficient Reason** — grounds God, indiscernibles and truths of fact alike.
- **Truths of reason**: opposite contradictory, **finite** analysis. **Truths of fact**: opposite conceivable, **infinite** analysis, completed only by God.
- **Complete individual concept** contains every predicate ever true of the individual.
- **Possible worlds** and **compossibility**; the **best** world = greatest variety under the simplest laws — *not* maximum comfort. Voltaire's *Candide* (1759) is **satire, not doctrine**.
- **Pre-established harmony**: three options — **influence** (Descartes) · **assistance** (occasionalism) · **harmony** (Leibniz, set once at creation). The **two clocks**.
- Freedom = **spontaneity + intelligence + contingency**; reasons **"incline without necessitating"** *(incliner sans nécessiter)*.
- **Hypothetical or moral necessity ≠ absolute or logical necessity** — the single most valuable distinction in the Leibniz half of this topic.

### I. God across the trio

| Descartes | Spinoza | Leibniz |
|---|---|---|
| **guarantees** knowledge | **is** the system | **selects** and harmonises |
| personal, transcendent creator | impersonal, immanent substance | necessary being, rational chooser |
| trademark + conservation + ontological | a priori, from the definition of substance | cosmological via the Principle of Sufficient Reason, plus a repaired ontological route |
| created freely; gave the will its scope | acts from the necessity of the divine nature; no will | chose freely among possibles, though determined by what is best |

- Mnemonic **G-I-S**: Guarantees · Is · Selects.
- Evaluative rider: Kant's objection that existence is not a real predicate bears on Descartes and Leibniz; it leaves Spinoza largely untouched, because he does not infer God from a concept of perfection.

### J. Mind–body and freedom — the ranking rail

| | Descartes | Spinoza | Leibniz |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mind–body | **interaction** (pineal gland) | **parallelism** (one thing, two attributes) | **pre-established harmony** |
| Mechanism | direct causal link | identity of the two orders | divine synchronisation at creation |
| Strength | matches lived experience | no interaction needed; economical | preserves plurality and internal agency |
| Weakness | unintelligible cross-substance causation | ordinary mental causation disappears | *ad hoc*; correspondence bought in advance |
| Freedom | libertarian: affirm, deny, withhold | acting from one's own understood nature | spontaneity + intelligence + contingency |

- **State the criterion, then rank.** Alternatives possible → Descartes. Self-determination → Spinoza. Responsible under determination → **Leibniz** (the examiner-safe 2024 verdict).
- Economy → Spinoza; fidelity to experience → Descartes; systematic consistency → Leibniz.

### K. Criticism → reply → residual matrix

| Target | Criticism | Reply | Residual |
|---|---|---|---|
| Descartes | the Circle | attended intuition vs remembered certainty | the proofs use extended reasoning |
| Descartes | interaction gap (Elisabeth, 1643) | the union is a **primitive notion** | a location, not a mechanism |
| Descartes | "substance" is equivocal | analogical: absolute of God, relative of creatures | Spinoza exploits exactly this |
| Spinoza | pantheism removes a personal God | morality rests on adequate knowledge, not command | providence and petition are lost |
| Spinoza | **acosmism** (Hegel) | modes are real **as expressions** | dependent individuality remains thin |
| Spinoza | determinism ends responsibility | freedom is adequate causation | alternatives never return |
| Spinoza | "determination is negation" makes the finite merely negative | finite things are positive modes, limited by other modes | the slogan is Hegel's universalisation of Letter 50 |
| Leibniz | harmony is *ad hoc* and unfalsifiable | the only route avoiding both influx and monism | the defence is comparative, not positive |
| Leibniz | "best possible world" — Voltaire | "best" = maximum variety, simplest laws | the perfection criterion is not independently testable |
| Leibniz | fixed complete concepts erase freedom | the opposite implies no contradiction | alternatives lie **across** worlds, not **within** this one |

### L. Exam craft — PYQ routes, directives, provenance and the final trap sheet

**Exact PYQ route map, 2018–2025 — fourteen primary-owned parts.**

| Year · part · marks | Route |
|---|---|
| 2018 Q1(b) · 10 | harmony → windowlessness → spontaneity + intelligence + contingency → hypothetical necessity → verdict |
| 2018 Q2(a) · 20 | shared definition → three enforcements → costs → "criterion, not discovery" |
| 2019 Q1(d) · 10 | five-step Meditation VI causal argument → limits → theological dependence |
| 2019 Q2(c) · 15 | P16 → three distinctions → ethical consequence → responsibility objection |
| 2020 Q1(b) · 10 | monadic structure → determination → three conditions → **first-person paragraph** |
| 2020 Q4(b) · 15 | P16 + P18 → four consequences → three criticisms with replies |
| 2022 Q2(c) · 15 | four-step monism → attributes and modes → **declared** pantheism verdict → acosmism |
| 2022 Q4(b) · 15 | doubt → *cogito* → *res cogitans* → mediated world → certainty vs substantiality |
| 2023 Q1(c) · 10 | interpret Def. 7 → stone analogy → adequate causation → concession |
| 2023 Q2(c) · 15 | three kinds of ideas → insufficiency → dispositional innateness → Locke's two tests → adjudication |
| 2024 Q2(b) · 15 | substance argument → ontological "in" → P18 → three objections and replies |
| 2024 Q3(c) · 15 | **announce criterion** → all three accounts → ranked verdict → the winner's cost |
| 2025 Q2(a) · 20 | five tenets → seven Cartesian stages → unifying logic → four vulnerabilities |
| 2025 Q2(b) · 15 | **Letter 50 provenance** → meaning → God, modes, knowledge → acosmism → ruling |

**Directive decoder.** Discuss = exposition + assessment, roughly 60:40. Critically discuss = assessment dominates; a verdict is compulsory. Comment = short, thesis-first, inside the author's own system. Explain = foreground the derivation. Substantiate your view = take a side. With your own comments = a signposted first-person quarter. "Whose account …?" = rank and defend. "What is the reason …?" = give a mechanism.

**Answer spines.** 10 marks **D-R-A-E** — Define, Reconstruct, Apply a named text, Evaluate. 15 marks **D-R-D-E-V** — Define, Reconstruct, Debate, Evaluate, Verdict. 20 marks **C-M-C** — Common premise, Mechanism of divergence, Comparative costs and conclusion.

**Provenance discipline.** Cite Spinoza structurally, never by page. "Determination is negation" = **Letter 50**, universalised by Hegel. "Ghost in the machine" = **Ryle, 1949**, not Descartes. *Candide* (1759) = **Voltaire's satire**, not Leibniz's doctrine. "Monads have no windows" and "incline without necessitating" are **standard renderings** — say so. *Cogito, ergo sum* is from the *Discourse* and *Principles* I.7, not the *Meditations*.

**Final trap sheet — ten lines.**

1. The *cogito* is not a syllogism, and it does not by itself prove a substantial soul.
2. The wax argument does not prove that bodies exist.
3. Descartes' "substance" is **analogical**; he concedes it in *Principles* I.51.
4. Thought and Extension are Spinozist **attributes**, not substances.
5. Spinoza's freedom is not indeterminism, and Spinoza is not an atheist.
6. Pantheism and acosmism are **opposite** charges.
7. Monads never causally interact, and not every monad apperceives.
8. Pre-established harmony is set **once at creation** — continuous adjustment is occasionalism.
9. Leibnizian certainty is **not** absolute necessity.
10. A comparative answer without an announced **criterion** remains descriptive.

**Memory chain — say it in fifteen seconds.** *One definition of substance, three degrees of strictness: three, one, infinitely many. God guarantees, is, selects. Mind and body interact, are one, are harmonised. Freedom is the will's assent, the intellect's adequacy, and spontaneity with intelligence and contingency. Every comparison needs a criterion.*

```ascii-master
   corrects an equivocation Descartes CONCEDES in Principles I.51; Leibniz's
   pluralism corrects Spinoza's absorption of individuals.
CAUTION 1: the Ethics appeared only after Spinoza's death, so seventeenth-
   century reaction to "Spinozism" often ran ahead of the text.
CAUTION 2: Leibniz's system must be assembled from the Discourse, the Theodicy,
   the Monadology and the correspondence together - which matters whenever an
   examiner quotes a single work.
```

```ascii-master
ADJUDICATION THAT EARNS THE MARK
   Locke DECISIVELY refutes OCCURRENT innatism - the doctrine that the mind is
   born consciously holding propositions.
   He does NOT as clearly refute DISPOSITIONAL innatism.
   But the burden then shifts to the rationalist to say what makes a
   disposition specifically INNATE rather than a general capacity to learn -
   and at that point the debate is genuinely unresolved.

   FIRST, then let Locke attack, then rule.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 11/43: The wax argument: the eight numbered steps

```ascii-master
================================================================================
1  TAKE THE CLEAREST POSSIBLE CASE OF A BODY - wax fresh from the honeycomb
      SENSIBLE CATALOGUE: taste of honey | scent of flowers | a colour |
      a shape | a size | hardness | coldness | a sound when rapped
        |
2  MOVE IT TO THE FIRE -> EVERY item in that catalogue is destroyed or
   replaced: taste and scent vanish, colour alters, shape and size are lost,
   it becomes liquid and hot, and it no longer sounds
        |
3  YET I JUDGE, WITHOUT THE LEAST HESITATION, THAT THE SAME WAX REMAINS
        |
4  THEREFORE what I take the wax to BE is not identical with any item at
   step 1, since all of them were replaced while the wax persisted
        |
5  NOR IS IT DELIVERED BY IMAGINATION: heat can give the wax an INDEFINITE
   number of shapes and sizes, and I cannot run through indefinitely many
   images.  THIS IS THE FORCE OF flexibile et mutabile ("flexible and
   changeable").
        |
6  THEREFORE the wax is grasped as an EXTENDED, FLEXIBLE, CHANGEABLE thing by
   the INTELLECT ALONE - by inspectio mentis, rendered "purely mental
   scrutiny" in the Cottingham-Stoothoff-Murdoch translation and "inspection
   of the mind" in Haldane-Ross
        |
7  FURTHER: at every stage - judging the wax present, doubting it, being
   deceived about it - I perceive MYSELF judging. Each perception of a body is
   ipso facto a perception of the mind that perceives, and a MORE CERTAIN one,
   because it survives even if the body does not exist
        |
8  THEREFORE (a) BODIES ARE KNOWN BY UNDERSTANDING, NOT BY SENSE
             (b) THE MIND IS notior - MORE EVIDENTLY KNOWN - THAN ANY BODY

PAIRED CASE IN THE SAME MEDITATION: "HATS AND COATS". From a window I say I
   SEE men crossing the square; strictly I see only hats and coats and JUDGE
   that men are underneath. What passes for seeing is in fact judging.
PRINTED SUB-TITLE OF MEDITATION II: "The nature of the human mind, and how it
   is better known than the body."
EXACT SUBTERMS TO USE: cera | inspectio mentis | sensus vs imaginatio vs
   intellectus | flexibile et mutabile | res extensa.
```

```ascii-master
   1 I have an idea of God: an infinite, perfect being.

     idea has OBJECTIVE reality.
   3 I, finite and imperfect, cannot be the adequate cause of an idea of an
     infinite perfect being.

     a craftsman stamps his mark on his work.

   I exist now; nothing in me gives me the power to sustain myself from one
   moment to the next; the cause of my continued existence must therefore
   ultimately be God.
   ANSWERS: what keeps me in existence?
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 16/43: The Cartesian Circle: objection, reply and residual

```ascii-master
================================================================================
THE OBJECTION  (ARNAULD, FOURTH OBJECTIONS)
   Descartes uses CLEAR AND DISTINCT PERCEPTION to prove that GOD exists;
   he then uses GOD'S VERACITY to guarantee that CLEAR AND DISTINCT
   PERCEPTION is reliable.  The guarantee therefore presupposes what it is
   meant to establish.

        criterion  ------ proves ------>  God
             ^                              |
             |                              |
             +-------- certifies -----------+

THE PICTURE IN ONE LINE: Descartes builds a bridge out of God, and the planks
   were cut from the far island he has not yet reached.

THE REPLY - TWO SITUATIONS MUST BE DISTINGUISHED
   (i)  A clear and distinct perception ACTUALLY BEFORE THE MIND compels
        assent and needs no external certificate. Some planks are solid enough
        to stand on while you are looking at them.
   (ii) What needs certifying is the perception merely REMEMBERED as having
        been clear, and any EXTENDED CHAIN of reasoning whose earlier steps
        are no longer in view.
   GOD SECURES CLASS (ii), NOT CLASS (i).

THE RESIDUAL - SAY THIS, OR THE ANSWER IS INCOMPLETE
   The proofs of God are NOT single-flash intuitions. They are arguments with
   premises, and by the time the conclusion is reached the earlier premises
   are being REMEMBERED rather than attended to. So the memory reply SOFTENS
   the objection without eliminating it.

COMPETING READINGS (optional depth, one clause at most)
   vicious-circle reading (Arnauld's line) | memory-guarantee reading |
   stronger anti-circle reading on which the cogito and simple intuitions are
   self-certifying while attended to.

CONSEQUENCE FOR EVERY LATER PANEL: whatever divine veracity underwrites -
   mathematics, memory, the external world, the conservation of created
   substances - inherits this vulnerability.
```

```ascii-master
================================================================================
THE ARGUMENT  [2019 Q1(d) is marked on these five steps]
   1 SENSORY IDEAS ARRIVE INVOLUNTARILY. I cannot decide not to feel heat when
     near the fire. So I possess a PASSIVE FACULTY of receiving them.
   2 A PASSIVE FACULTY REQUIRES AN ACTIVE CAUSE containing that reality
     formally or eminently.
   3 THE CANDIDATES ARE: myself, God directly, or bodies. It is not myself,
     because the ideas arrive without my willing them.
   4 I HAVE A GREAT NATURAL INCLINATION to refer them to bodies, and NO
     FACULTY by which God could have given me the means to correct it.
     If no bodies existed, God would be permitting an uncorrectable systematic
     error - that is, DECEIVING.
   5 THEREFORE MATERIAL THINGS EXIST AS EXTENDED REALITIES.

WHAT THE PROOF SECURES        |  WHAT IT DOES NOT SECURE
   the existence of body      |    the literal accuracy of any particular
   the reality of EXTENSION   |    colour, size, distance or sensible quality
                              |    -> the senses are reliable for what is
                              |       useful to the MIND-BODY UNION, not for
                              |       metaphysics; INTELLECT must correct them
        |
        v
EXCLUSION THAT IS ITSELF WORTH A MARK
   THE WAX ARGUMENT DOES NOT DO THIS WORK. It shows HOW bodies would be known;
   it says nothing about whether any EXIST. Saying so explicitly separates a
   controlled answer from a confused one.

STRUCTURE OF THE PROOF: THEOLOGICAL, NOT PERCEPTUAL. What does the work is not
   the vividness of experience but the IMPOSSIBILITY OF DIVINE DECEPTION.

INHERITED VULNERABILITY: the whole bridge carries Arnauld's Circle (Panel 12).
   The external world is only as secure as the divine proofs on which it rests.

ORDER TO MEMORISE: THINKING SELF -> GOD -> WORLD.
   TRAP: "Descartes proved the external world first, then God" inverts the
   entire architecture.
```

```ascii-master
THE ARGUMENT FOR REAL DISTINCTION (Meditation VI)
   1 I clearly and distinctly conceive myself as a thinking, non-extended
     thing, and body as an extended, non-thinking thing.

     to create as separate.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 20/43: Interaction, the union, error and Cartesian freedom

```ascii-master
================================================================================
THE PROBLEM
   RES COGITANS and RES EXTENSA SHARE NOTHING WHATEVER.
   And yet, in a human being: a decision to raise the arm -> the arm rises;
   a pin in the foot -> a FELT pain, not an observed injury.
        |
   PRINCESS ELISABETH OF BOHEMIA, correspondence of 1643:
   HOW can a thing whose entire nature is THOUGHT determine the animal spirits
   to move the body, when causation of motion seems to require contact and
   extension?  Causation appears to require some COMMUNITY between cause and
   effect; thought and extension have NONE.
        |
DESCARTES' TWO ANSWERS - KEEP THEM APART
   PHYSIOLOGICAL: the PINEAL GLAND (Passions of the Soul), chosen because it
      is a single UNPAIRED structure at the centre of the brain, whereas most
      brain structures are duplicated.
      >>> THIS IS A LOCATION, NOT A MECHANISM. <<<
   PHILOSOPHICAL: the mind-body UNION is a THIRD PRIMITIVE NOTION, alongside
      thought and extension, grasped through ORDINARY LIVING rather than
      through pure intellect. Trying to conceive it by intellect alone is
      precisely what makes it seem impossible.
        |
VERDICT: the reply PRESERVES THE PHENOMENON and ABANDONS THE MECHANISM.
   THIS VACANCY IS WHAT SPINOZA AND LEIBNIZ COMPETE TO FILL.
   The union is intimate, NOT a pilot in a ship - which is why we FEEL pain.
```

```ascii-master
ERROR AND FREEDOM (MEDITATION IV)
   THE WILL is INFINITE in scope: I can affirm or deny anything whatever.
   THE INTELLECT is FINITE: I perceive clearly only within limits.
   ERROR = the will affirming BEYOND what the intellect clearly perceives.
      -> error is not caused by God but by my misuse of a perfect faculty

      reducible to bodily mechanism.

      it is moved by evident reason rather than by ignorance or compulsion.

      between alternative possibilities, clear intellectual determination and
      divine preordination is contested and underexplained.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 22/43: Spinoza: one substance and its architecture

```ascii-master
================================================================================
THE ARGUMENT FOR MONISM  (Ethics I, more geometrico)
   1 DEFINITION. Substance = what is IN ITSELF and is CONCEIVED THROUGH
     ITSELF; its concept requires no other concept.
   2 NO SHARING. Two substances cannot share an attribute - if they did there
     would be nothing left by which to tell them apart QUA SUBSTANCE.
   3 NO FINITUDE. A finite substance would have to be limited by another OF
     THE SAME NATURE - which step 2 forbids. So substance is necessarily
     INFINITE.
   4 CONCLUSION. There can be only ONE substance, and it must possess ALL
     attributes: ABSOLUTELY INFINITE. This is God.
   COROLLARY: being in itself and conceived through itself, substance is
     SELF-CAUSED (causa sui): its essence involves existence.
        |
        v
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|      ONE ABSOLUTELY INFINITE, SELF-CAUSED SUBSTANCE = DEUS SIVE NATURA     |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
   INFINITELY MANY ATTRIBUTES, each expressing the WHOLE eternal and infinite
```

```ascii-master
MODE = an affection of substance; that which is IN ANOTHER and is CONCEIVED

   be or be conceived without God."  The "IN" is ONTOLOGICAL, NOT SPATIAL.

   absolute nature of an attribute; FINITE modes are determined by other
   finite modes in an endless series.

   comparative answer, because it removes the reason parallelism replaces
   interaction.
```

```ascii-master
THE DERIVATION  [2019 Q2(c): "what is, cannot be other than what it is"]
   1 God, or Nature, is the one self-caused substance, acting from the
     necessity of the divine nature alone.
   2 P16: infinitely many things follow from that necessity - modes follow
     from WHAT SUBSTANCE IS, as conclusions follow from premises.
   3 THEREFORE modes are not products of a discretionary decree; there is no
     deliberation, no selection among alternatives, no purpose.
   4 P29: contingency is a name for OUR IGNORANCE OF CAUSES, not an objective
     gap in the order.
   5 Finite modes are determined by OTHER FINITE MODES in an endless series
     WITHIN the one necessary order.
        |
        v
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 26/43: Spinoza: necessitarianism and the denial of contingency (continued)

```ascii-master
================================================================================
THREE DISTINCTIONS THAT MUST STAY CLEAN
   (i)   God is free NOT because God could have chosen otherwise but because
         God is determined by NOTHING EXTERNAL (Def. 7).
   (ii)  The necessity is LOGICAL-ONTOLOGICAL, not fatalistic compulsion by an
         external agent. There is no agent doing the pushing.
   (iii) Denying contingency is NOT denying causality; it is denying that the
         causal order could have run differently.
   ALSO: the inference is ONTOLOGICAL, NOT TEMPORAL. It is not a story in
         which God exists first and produces a world afterwards.
        |
        v
THREE CRITICISMS OF P16, WITH REPLIES AND RESIDUES
   necessity removes divine PERSONALITY  -> will and intellect are not
      faculties outside nature; anthropomorphic choice would LIMIT God
      RESIDUAL: providence, petition and worship are genuinely lost
   EVIL follows necessarily from God     -> good and evil express relations to
      human flourishing, not properties of infinite substance
      RESIDUAL: suffering still belongs, unredeemed, to the necessary order
   P16 ASSERTS MORE THAN THE DEFINITIONS PROVE -> an infinite cause must
      express itself infinitely
      RESIDUAL: the derivation of DETERMINATE FINITE modes remains the hardest
      step in Ethics I
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 27/43: Spinoza: 'determination is negation' - provenance and use

```ascii-master
================================================================================
PROVENANCE FIRST - THIS LINE IS ITSELF WORTH A MARK  [2025 Q2(b)]
   The exact Latin formula OMNIS DETERMINATIO EST NEGATIO IS NOT IN THE
   ETHICS.
   Spinoza writes DETERMINATIO NEGATIO EST in LETTER 50 TO JARIG JELLES,
   2 JUNE 1674.
   The universalised slogan is HEGEL'S rendering, and that is how it entered
   the textbook tradition.
   SAFE SENTENCE: "Spinoza's dictum, in Letter 50, that determination is
   negation - universalised by Hegel as omnis determinatio est negatio."
        |
        v
THE MEANING
   To DETERMINE something is to make it definite, finite, BOUNDED.
   To say a thing is THIS is to say it is NOT everything else.
   FINITUDE THEREFORE CONTAINS NEGATION.
        |
        v
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 28/43: Spinoza: 'determination is negation' - provenance and use (continued)

```ascii-master
================================================================================
THREE CONSEQUENCES - EXACTLY WHAT A "COMMENT" ANSWER NEEDS
   FOR GOD          absolutely infinite, bounded by nothing external, so God
                    involves NO negation whatever: PURE AFFIRMATION,
                    undetermined by anything outside
   FOR FINITE MODES each is limited by OTHER MODES OF THE SAME NATURE - a body
                    occupies this region and excludes others; an idea expresses
                    only a bounded causal perspective; their determinacy marks
                    DEPENDENCE
   FOR KNOWLEDGE    inadequate knowledge treats a finite thing IN ISOLATION;
   AND FREEDOM      adequate knowledge understands it THROUGH THE AFFIRMATIVE
                    WHOLE, seeing it SUB SPECIE AETERNITATIS - under the aspect
                    of eternity
        |
        v
HEGEL'S CRITICISM AND THE REPLY
   CRITICISM: if determination is negation, the finite becomes MERE negation,
      and the world vanishes into substance -> ACOSMISM.
   REPLY: what is negated is INDEPENDENT SELF-SUBSISTENCE, not reality as
      such; modes POSITIVELY EXPRESS divine power in determinate ways.
   VERDICT: the dictum illuminates why finite beings are dependent, but it
      becomes reductive if LIMITATION is mistaken for UNREALITY. Spinoza needs
      BOTH the negation of independence AND the affirmation of modal
      expression.
DIRECTIVE NOTE: "COMMENT" means short, pointed and THESIS-FIRST, stating what
   the sentence means INSIDE Spinoza's own system - never a general essay.
```

```ascii-master
================================================================================
THE PROPOSITIONS
   II, P7   The ORDER AND CONNECTION OF IDEAS is the same as the ORDER AND
            CONNECTION OF THINGS.
   II, P13  The human MIND IS THE IDEA OF THE HUMAN BODY - so the mind's
            adequacy tracks the body's power of acting.
        |
        v
THE PICTURE
   IDEA_1 ------> IDEA_2 ------> IDEA_3     causation stays INSIDE Thought
     ||             ||             ||       the SAME modes, the SAME order
   BODY_1 ------> BODY_2 ------> BODY_3     causation stays INSIDE Extension
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 30/43: Spinoza: parallelism dissolves the mind-body problem (continued)

```ascii-master
================================================================================
   A decision to raise the arm and the arm's movement are ONE AND THE SAME
   MODE, expressed under Thought and under Extension respectively.
        |
        v
THE DISSOLUTION
   The mind-body problem was GENERATED by Descartes' assumption that mind and
   body are DIFFERENT SUBSTANCES. Remove the assumption and the problem does
   not get SOLVED - it stops ARISING. Spinoza does not build a bridge; he
   denies there is a gap.
        |
        v
THE COST - STATE IT HONESTLY
   If no idea can cause a bodily motion, ORDINARY MENTAL CAUSATION disappears.
   Thought and Extension are each CAUSALLY CLOSED, so critics say the
   correspondence is secured at the price of making mind explanatorily idle.
   SPINOZA'S ANSWER: the mind is not idle WITHIN Thought - an adequate idea
   genuinely causes further adequate ideas - but the ordinary intuition that a
   decision moves a limb is indeed abandoned.
        |
        v
TRAPS
   "two parallel trains running alongside" quietly RESTORES two things.
      Spinoza's claim is stronger: ONE thing, described completely TWICE.
   "God continually adjusts the two series" is OCCASIONALISM, a rival
      seventeenth-century position that Spinoza's monism makes unnecessary.
   Making II, P13 a CAUSAL claim ("the body produces the mind") reinstates
      exactly the cross-attribute causation the doctrine excludes.
POSITION IN THE 2024 RANKING: metaphysically the most ECONOMICAL account, and
   the one that most decisively removes the problem - at the price of
   necessitarianism and the loss of free will.
```

```ascii-master
| FIRST  | imaginatio /     | (a) EXPERIENTIA VAGA -    | INADEQUATE,      |
|        | opinio           |     random experience     | mutilated and    |

|        |                  | (b) EX SIGNIS - hearsay,  | II, P41: THE     |

| SECOND | ratio            | COMMON NOTIONS (notiones  | ADEQUATE,        |
|        |                  | communes) - what is       | necessarily true |
|        |                  | EQUALLY IN THE PART AND   | (II, P41)        |
|        |                  | IN THE WHOLE              |                  |

|        |                  | to adequate knowledge of  | (V, P25)         |

SPINOZA'S OWN EXAMPLE - THE FOURTH PROPORTIONAL (1 : 2 :: 3 : ?) = 6
   MERCHANT      multiplies the second by the third and divides by the first,
                 because he was taught the rule or has seen it work -> FIRST
   MATHEMATICIAN demonstrates it from the common property of proportionals
                 (Euclid VII.19); adequate but DISCURSIVE -> SECOND
   ANYONE, HERE  sees AT A GLANCE that the fourth is 6, grasping the ratio in
                 the essences themselves, IN ONE ACT -> THIRD
   THE THIRD KIND IS SCIENTIA INTUITIVA - name it in full, never just
   "intuition".  MNEMONIC "M-M-S": Merchant, Mathematician, Seer.
   THE THREE KINDS ARE THREE ONTOLOGICAL POSITIONS OF THE KNOWER, NOT THREE
   DEGREES OF CONFIDENCE.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 32/43: Spinoza: three kinds of knowledge and freedom (continued)

```ascii-master
================================================================================
THE ARGUMENT THAT MAKES KNOWLEDGE INTO FREEDOM
   1 Everything follows necessarily from the nature of God or Substance.
   2 An idea is ADEQUATE when understood through its cause - through its place
     in that necessary order.
   3 BONDAGE (servitus) = being determined by causes EXTERNAL to my nature,
     which is exactly the condition of one whose ideas are inadequate, since
     inadequate ideas are the TRACES OF OTHER BODIES in mine.
   4 FREEDOM (I, Def. 7) = being determined by MY OWN nature alone.
   5 Adequate ideas ARE the mind acting from its own nature, for in having
     them the mind is the ADEQUATE CAUSE of what follows from them.
   6 THEREFORE moving from the first kind to the second and third IS moving
     from bondage to freedom. KNOWLEDGE IS NOT A MEANS TO FREEDOM; IT IS
     FREEDOM.
   7 At the limit the third kind sees things SUB SPECIE AETERNITATIS and
     generates the INTELLECTUAL LOVE OF GOD (amor Dei intellectualis,
     V, P32 Cor.) - the mind's highest good and blessedness (beatitudo).
        |
        v
STRIVING AND THE AFFECTS - RETAINED ONLY AS FAR AS THEY EXPLAIN FREEDOM
   III, P6-P7  each thing strives to persevere in its being (CONATUS), and
      this striving IS THE ACTUAL ESSENCE of the thing. In a human being,
      conscious striving appears as DESIRE.
   PASSIVE AFFECTS arise when the person is only a PARTIAL cause under
      external determination.  ACTIVE AFFECTS arise from adequate ideas and
      register an INCREASE IN THE POWER OF ACTING.
FREE WILL IS AN ILLUSION: we are conscious of our DESIRES but ignorant of the
   CAUSES that determine them - a thrown STONE, if conscious, would believe it
   chose to fly.
ONLY GOD IS ABSOLUTELY FREE. Human freedom is RELATIVE and ACHIEVED.
LIVE WEAKNESS TO CONCEDE: II, P43 - "he who has a true idea knows at the same
   time that he has a true idea"; truth is the standard of itself and of the
   false (verum index sui). But SELF-CERTIFICATION is exactly what confident
   IMAGINATION also feels like.
CONCEDE ALSO: alternative possibilities never return, and even the achievement
   of freedom is itself necessitated.
```

```ascii-master
THE ARGUMENT TO SIMPLES  (Monadology Sec.1-3)
   1 Extension is essentially DIVISIBLE.
   2 Whatever is divisible is a COMPOSITE, an aggregate of parts.
   3 An aggregate derives its reality from the reality of its constituents;
     a heap of heaps of heaps, with no grains anywhere, is no heap.
   4 THEREFORE extended things cannot be ultimate. There must be SIMPLE
     substances, and these cannot themselves be extended.
   NOTE: this refutes SPINOZA too - if extension cannot be a possible essence
   of substance, Extension cannot be an attribute in Spinoza's sense.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 34/43: Leibniz: monads, hierarchy and indiscernibles (continued)

```ascii-master
================================================================================
                    illustration: the ROAR OF THE SEA - we hear the whole
                    roar, so we must in some way perceive each wave.
      APPERCEPTION = conscious reflexive awareness. TRAP: it must NOT be
                    attributed to every monad.
   Each monad represents the WHOLE universe from its own point of view - "a
   perpetual living mirror of the universe".
        |
        v
TWO DOCTRINES THAT MAKE THIS WORK
   NO WINDOWS (Monadology Sec.7): "Monads have no windows through which
      anything could come in or go out" - as it is USUALLY RENDERED; the
      French is Les Monades n'ont point de fenetres. No causal influx enters;
      all change arises INTERNALLY from appetition.
   IDENTITY OF INDISCERNIBLES: no two monads are exactly alike, because God
      would have had NO SUFFICIENT REASON to create both rather than one.
      -> INDIVIDUALITY IS GUARANTEED, NOT MERELY PERMITTED. This is precisely
         what Spinoza's system cannot deliver, and it is Leibniz's motive.
        |
        v
AND WHAT ABOUT BODIES?
   An organism is coordinated by a DOMINANT MONAD together with an ORGANIC
   AGGREGATE of subordinate monads - coordination, NOT causal control through
   exchanged signals.
   Extended bodies are WELL-FOUNDED PHENOMENA (phaenomena bene fundata),
   grounded in ordered monadic aggregates: NOT ultimate substances, but
   NEITHER arbitrary private illusions. The adjective "WELL-FOUNDED" is doing
   the work; dropping it turns Leibniz into a subjective idealist, which he is
   not.
CONSEQUENCE: interaction is impossible IN PRINCIPLE, so the mind-body question
   must be answered some other way - see Panel 23.
```

```ascii-master
POSSIBLE WORLDS AND THE BEST WORLD
   God's understanding contains infinitely many possible individuals. Not all
   possibles are COMPOSSIBLE - capable of existing together - so a possible
   world is a MAXIMAL COMPOSSIBLE SET.
   God actualises the world of GREATEST PERFECTION = THE GREATEST VARIETY OF
   PHENOMENA GOVERNED BY THE SIMPLEST LAWS. "Best" does NOT mean maximum human
   comfort or the absence of suffering.
   THEODICY, as far as this topic owns it: evil is permitted because a world
   containing it is, overall, the most perfect compossible WHOLE; perfection is
   a property of the SYSTEM, not of each part alone.
   VOLTAIRE, CANDIDE (1759): satire and RECEPTION, never doctrine.
        |
        v
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 37/43: Leibniz: sufficient reason, worlds, harmony, freedom (continued 2)

```ascii-master
================================================================================
PRE-ESTABLISHED HARMONY - LEIBNIZ'S OWN THREE OPTIONS
   1 INFLUENCE   a rod between the two clocks   = DESCARTES' interaction
                 -> rejected as unintelligible
   2 ASSISTANCE  a keeper adjusting them at     = OCCASIONALISM
                 every moment                    -> rejected as unworthy of a
                                                    perfect craftsman
   3 HARMONY     perfect construction AT THE     = LEIBNIZ
                 OUTSET, so both keep the same
                 time with no connection
   TRAP: describing harmony as continuous divine intervention converts Leibniz
   into an occasionalist. It is set ONCE, AT CREATION.
        |
        v
FREEDOM = THREE CONDITIONS, JOINTLY NECESSARY
   SPONTANEITY   the action springs from the agent's own nature; no external
                 compulsion - GUARANTEED BY WINDOWLESSNESS ITSELF
   INTELLIGENCE  the agent deliberates and acts for perceived reasons, not by
                 blind impact - this distinguishes a rational spirit from a
                 bare monad
   CONTINGENCY   the actual world is one of infinitely many possible worlds;
                 the opposite of the act implies no contradiction
   MECHANISM: reasons INCLINE WITHOUT NECESSITATING
   (incliner sans necessiter) - the determination is MORAL, not LOGICAL.
RESIDUAL TO CONCEDE: since the complete concept of the ACTUAL agent is fixed
   and this world is actualised, alternatives lie ACROSS worlds, not WITHIN
   this one; a libertarian will call that contingency merely epistemic.
VERDICT: COHERENT COMPATIBILISM, NOT INDETERMINISTIC FREEDOM.
TRAPS: not a libertarian | not a fatalist | not an occasionalist | Candide is
   satire | spontaneity ALONE is not the doctrine.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 41/43: PYQ routes, directives, spine and the conclusion (continued)

```ascii-master
================================================================================
DIRECTIVE DECODER
   DISCUSS                    exposition + assessment, roughly 60:40
   CRITICALLY DISCUSS/EXAMINE assessment DOMINATES; a verdict is compulsory
   COMMENT                    short, pointed, thesis-first, inside the
                              author's own system
   EXPLAIN                    foreground the DERIVATION, not a paraphrase
   SUBSTANTIATE YOUR VIEW     take a side and defend it with textual grounds
   WITH YOUR OWN COMMENTS     a signposted FIRST-PERSON final quarter
   "WHOSE ACCOUNT ...?"       rank all three against a stated criterion and
                              DEFEND the ranking
   "WHAT IS THE REASON ...?"  give a MECHANISM, not a table

ANSWER SPINES AND LENGTH BANDS
   10 MARKS  D-R-A-E    Define -> Reconstruct -> Apply a named text ->
                        Evaluate            about 150-200 words
   15 MARKS  D-R-D-E-V  Define -> Reconstruct -> Debate -> Evaluate ->
                        Verdict             about 250-300 words
   20 MARKS  C-M-C      Common premise -> Mechanism of divergence ->
                        Comparative costs and conclusion   about 340-400 words
   PARAGRAPH CONTROL: CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION.
```

```ascii-master
   Cite Spinoza STRUCTURALLY (I, Def. 7; I, P15; I, P16; I, P18; I, P29;
      II, P7; II, P13; II, P40 Sch. 2; V, P32 Cor.) - translation-invariant.
   "Determination is negation" = LETTER 50, 2 June 1674; universalised by
      HEGEL. NOT a sentence of the Ethics.
   "The ghost in the machine" = GILBERT RYLE, The Concept of Mind, 1949.
      NOT Descartes.
   CANDIDE (1759) = VOLTAIRE'S SATIRE. NOT Leibniz's doctrine.
   "Monads have no windows" and "incline without necessitating" are STANDARD
      RENDERINGS - say so.
   "cogito, ergo sum" is from the DISCOURSE and PRINCIPLES I.7, not the
      Meditations.
   inspectio mentis = "purely mental scrutiny" (Cottingham-Stoothoff-Murdoch)
      or "inspection of the mind" (Haldane-Ross) - name the edition or
      paraphrase.

FINAL TRAP SHEET - TEN LINES
   1  The cogito is not a syllogism and does not by itself prove a soul.
   2  The wax argument does not prove that bodies exist.
   3  Descartes' "substance" is ANALOGICAL - Principles I.51.
   4  Thought and Extension are Spinozist ATTRIBUTES, not substances.
   5  Spinoza's freedom is not indeterminism, and Spinoza is not an atheist.
   6  Pantheism and acosmism are OPPOSITE charges.
   7  Monads never interact, and not every monad apperceives.
   8  Harmony is set ONCE at creation; continuous adjustment is occasionalism.
   9  Leibnizian certainty is NOT absolute necessity.
   10 A comparative answer without a declared CRITERION stays descriptive.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 43/43: PYQ routes, directives, spine and the conclusion (continued 3)

```ascii-master
================================================================================
QUALIFIED CONCLUSION
   All three men accept that reason can reach a necessary intelligible order,
   and all three inherit ONE definition of substance. The differences are
   produced by how strictly that definition is enforced: loosely, giving three
   substances and an interaction Descartes cannot explain; strictly, giving one
   substance and a world in which individuality and alternatives are lost;
   strictly plus simplicity, giving infinitely many substances whose agreement
   must be arranged by God in advance.
   Judged by EXPLANATORY ECONOMY Spinoza wins; by FIDELITY TO EXPERIENCE
   Descartes wins; by SYSTEMATIC CONSISTENCY Leibniz wins.
   THAT NO SINGLE CRITERION SELECTS ONE ANSWER IS ITSELF THE STRONGEST
   EVIDENCE THAT WHAT DESCARTES BEQUEATHED WAS A PSEUDO-PROBLEM RATHER THAN A
   PROBLEM - AND IT IS WHY THE NEXT SYLLABUS ITEM, EMPIRICISM, BEGINS BY
   ATTACKING THE INNATE RESOURCES ON WHICH THE WHOLE PROGRAMME RESTS.
```
